The start and deadline were recorded before checker implementation or evidence
collection. The accepted scope ends with a reproducible `go` or precise
`no_go` packet without extending the deadline or reducing deliverables.

## Frozen evidence contract

| Field | Frozen value |
|-------|--------------|
| `research_date` | `2026-07-14` |
| Pinned repository revision | `7f8c17369e15a359c6e24eea844c79a2d47d3bab` |
| Platform authority | Current official OpenAI documentation only |
| Project authority | Tracked repository source at the pinned revision only |
| Derived project evidence | Generated payloads, caches, and installed state may reveal drift but never redefine tracked source |
| Surface rule | `cli`, `desktop_app`, `app_server`, and `non_interactive` are independent records; evidence is never transferred between them |

Q5 of the accepted Design Concept controls any earlier ambiguity: official
OpenAI documentation is exclusive authority for external platform facts, and
repository evidence is authoritative only for SpecKit Pro project facts.
Installed or cached observations cannot establish platform facts or silently
change the tracked contract.

### Evidence classes and claim labels

| Evidence or claim class | Use in this research |
|-------------------------|----------------------|
| `platform_fact` | A claim stated by a current official OpenAI source, with URL, exact locator, retrieval date, surface, documented scope, applicability, conflict status, and invalidation trigger |
| `project_fact` | A claim established by a repository-relative source at the pinned revision, with its producer/consumer evidence role |
| `reasonable_inference` | A bounded interpretation that cites supporting evidence and is never presented as documented platform behavior |
| `proposed_policy` | A downstream SpecKit Pro choice, not current platform or production behavior |
| `unverified_assumption` | A claim whose authoritative evidence is absent, stale, unavailable, or unverifiable |
| `environment_observation` | A sanitized cache or installed-state fact observed during this workday; never authority for tracked source or general platform behavior |
| `conflict` | Evidence that disagrees and is assigned an explicit terminal disposition |

Each provenance record carries a stable evidence ID, evidence class, claim
classification, exact locator, observation or retrieval date, surface,
feature, documented scope, applicability, conflict status, and concrete
invalidation triggers. Official records additionally carry an official OpenAI
URL. Project records additionally carry a repository-relative path, the pinned
revision, and an evidence role. Missing documented scope is recorded as the
literal `not_stated`; silence is never converted into support.

### Source and surface isolation

- `tracked_source` is the immutable project baseline for this research.
- `cached_source` is a derived logical observation and may only report drift.
- `installed_state` is a separate derived logical observation and may only
  report drift.
- Every agent receives one independent record for each of those three evidence
  classes.
- Each Codex surface receives its own `documented`, `undocumented`, or
  `not_applicable` record. `not_applicable` requires official evidence for the
  same feature and surface; otherwise the value is `undocumented`.
- A cache or installed mismatch records `mismatch_status`, impact, and a
  `defect_owner`; it does not mutate the tracked route or contract.

### Freshness and invalidation

Official and environment evidence can support a passing completion check only
when retrieved or observed between `started_at` and `deadline_at`. Project
evidence must match the pinned repository revision. Every record names concrete
invalidation triggers, including source revision, documentation, model-catalog,
client-version, payload, cache, or installed-state drift as applicable.

If a source becomes unavailable or unverifiable, or a trigger fires, the claim
is revalidated or reclassified from fact to `unverified_assumption` or
`conflict`. The affected completion check cannot pass until that happens. A
trigger after `go` invalidates the affected admission evidence and downstream
results until refresh and re-admission.

### Sanitization

Published cache and installed observations use logical locators only. This
record excludes absolute or home paths, usernames, hostnames, credentials,
tokens, secrets, unrelated configuration, and raw instruction contents from
local derived state. Only the fields needed for model/effort, version, surface,
instruction/contract hashes, mismatch ownership, and observation date may be
retained.

### Conflict terminal rules

| Conflict state | Meaning |
|----------------|---------|
| `none` | Evidence is consistent within its declared authority and scope |
| `resolved_by_authority` | The frozen authority rule determines the supported claim; conflicting evidence remains cited |
| `blocking_no_go` | A G56R-001 obligation cannot be established before the deadline |
| `nonblocking_deferred` | The unresolved question is outside G56R-001, has an exact owner, impact, and follow-up, and supports no G56R-001 conclusion |

No conflict remains unlabeled. Any unclassified unknown or missing G56R-001
obligation forces `no_go`; a classified G56R-002 or G56R-003 unknown may
coexist with `go` only when all G56R-001 checks pass.

### Research non-goals

This spike does not probe executable capabilities, run candidates or fixtures,
score or qualify routes, select preferred models, order fallbacks, fix source
defects, or mutate agents, prompts, installers, payloads, cache, installed
state, defaults, versions, or release artifacts. Installation availability is
`unresolved_g56r_002`; qualification and ordering belong to G56R-003. A local
absence never excludes a project-eligible candidate.

## Official OpenAI platform evidence

The official Codex pages below were retrieved on `2026-07-14`. Their canonical
`developers.openai.com` URLs currently redirect into OpenAI's Codex learning
site; the canonical URLs are retained so every platform claim has an
`openai.com` authority. A page describing a model or field establishes only the
documented product contract. It does not establish that a model is installed,
enabled for this account, or usable through a particular local client.

| Evidence ID | Feature | Official OpenAI URL and exact locator | Documented scope | Surface applicability | Conflict state | Invalidation trigger |
|-------------|---------|---------------------------------------|------------------|-----------------------|----------------|----------------------|
| `OAI-20260714-MODELS` | Model identifiers and selection | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/models` — **Recommended models**, **Choose a model** | The current Codex catalog names `gpt-5.6`, `gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, `gpt-5.6-luna`, `gpt-5.5`, `gpt-5.4`, `gpt-5.4-mini`, and `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` in its recommendation and other-model sections; the CLI and `codex exec` accept an explicit model choice. | `cli`: documented; `desktop_app`: documented by the page's model-picker guidance; `app_server`: defer to `OAI-20260714-APP-MODELS`; `non_interactive`: documented | `none` | The page's recommended or other-model catalog, selection syntax, or surface guidance changes. |
| `OAI-20260714-REASONING` | Reasoning controls | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/models` — **Pick a reasoning effort**; `https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-reference` — `model_reasoning_effort` | Codex exposes model-dependent reasoning choices. The configuration value is one of `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, or `xhigh`; product controls such as Max or Ultra are not assumed to be portable configuration strings. | `cli`: documented; `desktop_app`: documented model-picker control; `app_server`: supported values are discovered through `model/list`, not inferred; `non_interactive`: documented through shared configuration and model flags | `none` | The value set, model-dependent support, model picker, or configuration schema changes. |
| `OAI-20260714-AGENTS` | Custom-agent fields | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/subagents` — **Custom agents**, **Custom agent file schema**; `https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-reference` — `agents.<name>.config_file`, `agents.<name>.description`, `agents.<name>.nickname_candidates` | A standalone custom-agent TOML requires `name`, `description`, and `developer_instructions`; it may set `nickname_candidates`, `model`, `model_reasoning_effort`, `sandbox_mode`, `mcp_servers`, and `skills.config`. Root configuration may point a named agent at a TOML layer and provide selection guidance or nicknames. | `cli`: documented local client; `desktop_app`: documented local client; `app_server`: `undocumented` for authoring/loading custom agents; `non_interactive`: `undocumented` for spawning a named custom agent | `none` | The standalone schema, root `agents.*` schema, or stated local-client availability changes. |
| `OAI-20260714-APP-MODELS` | Capability discovery | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/app-server` — **Models / List models (`model/list`)**, **Model provider capabilities (`modelProvider/capabilities/read`)**, **Experimental features (`experimentalFeature/list`)** | App Server can enumerate available models and their supported/default reasoning efforts, visibility, upgrade relationship, input modalities, personality support, and default status; provider capability and experimental-feature methods expose additional bounds. | `cli`: `undocumented`; `desktop_app`: `undocumented`; `app_server`: documented; `non_interactive`: `undocumented` | `none` | App Server method names, response fields, provider bounds, or feature stages change. |
| `OAI-20260714-APP-EVENTS` | Reroute and runtime events | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/app-server` — **Events / Turn events**, `model/rerouted`, `model/safetyBuffering/updated` | App Server emits thread/turn lifecycle events, model and safety-buffer updates, model verification, token usage, item events, and a reroute event carrying thread, turn, source model, destination model, and reason. | `cli`: `undocumented` as this event schema; `desktop_app`: `undocumented` as this event schema; `app_server`: documented; `non_interactive`: `undocumented` as this event schema | `none` | Event names, payload fields, transport contract, or experimental gating changes. |
| `OAI-20260714-OTEL` | Telemetry | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-advanced` — **Observability and telemetry** | Opt-in OpenTelemetry records can cover conversation starts, API requests, server-sent or WebSocket events, user prompts, tool approval decisions, and tool results, with client/environment/model/sandbox metadata and metrics. Prompt contents require a separate opt-in. | `cli`: documented local-client configuration; `desktop_app`: documented through shared local configuration; `app_server`: `undocumented` as an App Server client contract; `non_interactive`: documented through shared local configuration | `none` | OTel event names, metadata, redaction defaults, exporter configuration, or surface guidance changes. |
| `OAI-20260714-EXEC-JSONL` | Non-interactive output | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/noninteractive` — **Make output machine-readable**, `--json`, `--output-schema` | `codex exec --json` writes JSONL events including thread, turn, item, and error records; item records cover messages, reasoning, commands, file changes, MCP calls, web searches, and plan updates. A JSON Schema may constrain the final response. | `cli`: `not_applicable` to interactive output; `desktop_app`: `not_applicable`; `app_server`: `not_applicable` to the `codex exec` stream; `non_interactive`: documented | `none` | `codex exec` flags, event types, item types, stdout/stderr behavior, or output-schema behavior changes. |
| `OAI-20260714-CYBER-REROUTE` | Safety rerouting | `https://developers.openai.com/codex/cyber-safety` — **How it works**, **False positives** | OpenAI may reroute traffic classified as higher-risk cybersecurity activity and surface the responding model in product notices or request logs. This source explains policy behavior but does not define a stable candidate route or fallback order. | `cli`: documented notice; `desktop_app`: page states broader client messaging but does not define a desktop event schema; `app_server`: event shape is documented separately by `OAI-20260714-APP-EVENTS`; `non_interactive`: `undocumented` | `none` | Safety policy, affected-model description, notice coverage, or reroute reporting changes. |

The manifest retains `OAI-20260714-AGENTS` as this human-readable umbrella and
adds one explicitly scoped record per surface with the suffixes `-CLI`,
`-DESKTOP-APP`, `-APP-SERVER`, and `-NON-INTERACTIVE`. Each agent surface record
references only its matching scoped evidence; no support transfers between
surfaces.

### Independent surface records

`documented` below means the cited page expressly covers the named surface and
feature. `undocumented` means the official evidence reviewed here does not say;
it is not evidence of absence. `not_applicable` is used only where the source
defines a different interface rather than merely remaining silent.

| Surface record ID | Surface | Model and effort selection | Custom agents | Capability discovery | Telemetry | Reroute evidence | Machine-readable run output | Applicability disposition |
|-------------------|---------|----------------------------|---------------|----------------------|-----------|------------------|-----------------------------|---------------------------|
| `SURFACE-CLI-20260714` | `cli` | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-MODELS`, `OAI-20260714-REASONING`) | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-AGENTS`) | `undocumented` | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-OTEL`) | `documented` product notice (`OAI-20260714-CYBER-REROUTE`), but no App Server event transfer | `not_applicable` to an interactive run | Supports only the named CLI claims. |
| `SURFACE-DESKTOP-20260714` | `desktop_app` | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-MODELS`, `OAI-20260714-REASONING`) | `documented` local client (`OAI-20260714-AGENTS`) | `undocumented` | `documented` through shared local configuration (`OAI-20260714-OTEL`) | `undocumented` as a stable desktop event or notice schema | `not_applicable` | Supports only app model controls, local-agent loading, and shared-config telemetry. |
| `SURFACE-APP-SERVER-20260714` | `app_server` | `documented` by runtime metadata (`OAI-20260714-APP-MODELS`) | `undocumented` | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-APP-MODELS`) | `undocumented` as a client-facing telemetry contract | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-APP-EVENTS`) | `not_applicable` to the `codex exec` stream; App Server has its own JSON-RPC transport | Supports only the listed App Server methods and event payloads. |
| `SURFACE-NONINTERACTIVE-20260714` | `non_interactive` | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-MODELS`, `OAI-20260714-REASONING`) | `undocumented` | `undocumented` | `documented` through shared local configuration (`OAI-20260714-OTEL`) | `undocumented` | `documented` (`OAI-20260714-EXEC-JSONL`) | Supports only `codex exec` selection, shared configuration, and output claims. |

The official model catalog supplies eligible identifier hypotheses, not an
installation snapshot. In particular, no `model/list` call is made in this
spike. G56R-002 owns runtime model/capability discovery, installation-time
availability, and binding that snapshot to later admission evidence.

## Active route-policy integration inventory

This inventory is frozen at repository revision
`7f8c17369e15a359c6e24eea844c79a2d47d3bab` and was observed on
`2026-07-14`. `C10` means the ten current Codex agents. `P2` means
`consensus-synthesizer` and `gate-validator`. `Claude11` means the eleven
corresponding Claude agents, excluding the Codex-only
`autopilot-fast-helper`. The tracked and generated plugin version is `2.19.1`.

An ID containing `<agent>` expands to one distinct stable entry per named file;
it is not a wildcard entry in the manifest. This keeps each physical producer
or derived file present exactly once while preserving an agent-centric JSON
projection. Every derived entry names a canonical input, and every arrow below
is reciprocal in the downstream entry.

| Finding ID | Conflict state | Evidence | Impact | Owner and required follow-up |

| `INV-DEFECT-CONSENSUS-ROUTE` | `nonblocking_deferred` | `RP-SKILL-CODEX-AUTO` requires a `consensus-synthesizer` worker while the canonical Codex source set, installer, payload, cache, and installed set contain no such production route. | The current consumer cannot bind that named worker to an installed custom-agent route. No inventory defect is repaired here. Each is project evidence only, supports no
platform claim, and is downstream-owned without weakening the current tracked
source baseline.

## Per-agent source observations

Each row below represents three independent evidence records. The tracked
record uses `surface=repository`, the cache record uses

## Current Codex agent semantic contracts

These contracts are derived from the complete decoded instruction bodies at the pinned revision. Each hard boundary preserves permitted behavior, prohibited behavior, and its stop or escalation condition. Production route tuples are immutable observations, not candidate rankings. Contract hashes are bound in the next ordered step.

### `analyze-executor`

- Contract ID: `agent-contract/analyze-executor/v1`
- Instruction source: `speckit-pro/codex-agents/analyze-executor.toml`
- Instruction hash: `sha256:771a5b9075240abf72b92449dae9960a03b8873e516468a13b7c6da17245f64c`
- Contract hash: `sha256:82b7ffc6fd0bcdfc6bbe2b508a9addf76640fa322ca98be1916818dc3619cc18`
- Immutable production route: `gpt-5.5` / `xhigh` / `workspace-write`

| Contract field | Derived semantic value |
|----------------|------------------------|
| `role_boundary` | Permitted: run `$speckit-analyze`, parse every severity, research and remediate every finding Prohibited: skip or defer findings, or act outside analysis remediation Stop/escalate: after two remediation loops, or on low-confidence, conflicting, or security-sensitive findings, return categorized unresolved items for consensus |
| `authorization_boundaries` | Permitted: apply evidence-backed fixes to `spec.md`, `plan.md`, or `tasks.md` Prohibited: decide unresolved consensus questions, conduct HITL scoping, or claim gate approval Stop/escalate: return unresolved authority to the parent orchestrator Approval conditions and authorized approver: only the parent orchestrator may authorize expanded work; no platform approver is named |
| `safety_requirements` | Permitted: bound remediation to two loops and preserve security escalation Prohibited: invoke `$grill-me`, suppress findings, or bypass verification Stop/escalate: classify security-keyword, low-confidence, and still-open findings as unresolved for consensus |
| `grounding_requirements` | Permitted: use capability-first codebase, external, and project-context evidence and cite each fix and external fact Prohibited: assert an ungrounded fix Stop/escalate: state when no source grounds a claim and return it unresolved |
| `mutation_policy` | Permitted: edit only the affected `spec.md`, `plan.md`, or `tasks.md` Prohibited: mutate unrelated artifacts or mark unresolved work complete Stop/escalate: stop mutation when confidence stays low or two loops are exhausted |
| `tool_requirements` | Permitted: invoke the analysis workflow, use runner `count-markers` in findings mode, and use task-fit evidence capabilities or documented fallbacks Prohibited: estimate counts or omit deterministic verification Stop/escalate: use local, native, or repository fallback when an installed capability is unavailable |
| `skill_requirements` | Permitted: invoke `$speckit-analyze` Prohibited: invoke `$grill-me` or an unrelated workflow Stop/escalate: return interactive scoping to the parent orchestrator |
| `mcp_requirements` | Permitted: select an installed MCP only when task fit supports it; local, native, and repository evidence are valid fallbacks Prohibited: infer any required or MCP-only behavior from source silence Stop/escalate: report a missing capability rather than fabricate an MCP requirement |
| `sandbox_expectations` | Permitted: write within the named analysis-remediation artifact scope under `workspace-write` Prohibited: write outside that scope or infer external-write authority Stop/escalate: return any broader access requirement to the parent orchestrator |
| `output_contract` | Permitted: return `## Analyze Result` with exact finding counts, remediations and citations, modified files, zero/open verification, unresolved categories, and errors Prohibited: recommend next steps, omit findings, or claim unsupported closure Stop/escalate: return errors and unresolved items explicitly |
| `supported_client_assumptions` | Permitted: assume only the installed Codex TOML custom-agent runtime referenced by the source Prohibited: claim independent CLI, desktop-app, App Server, or non-interactive support Stop/escalate: require surface-specific evidence before making any broader client claim |
| `representative_tasks` | `Run analyze and remediate all severities`; `Re-run and prove zero findings`; `Escalate categorized residual findings` |

| `authorization_boundaries` | Permitted: provide advisory text within the supplied prompt Prohibited: decide gates, choose consensus winners, approve or reject edits, or substitute for an executor or analyst Stop/escalate: return any decision to the parent Approval conditions and authorized approver: none is delegated; the parent retains every decision |

| `skill_requirements` | Permitted: use no skill invocation Prohibited: invoke any skill Stop/escalate: return the bounded prompt-only result instead |
| `mcp_requirements` | Permitted: use no MCP Prohibited: invoke or depend on any MCP Stop/escalate: return the bounded prompt-only result instead |
| `sandbox_expectations` | Permitted: remain read-only, with no filesystem access Prohibited: read or write files despite the runtime sandbox Stop/escalate: stop if the job cannot be completed from prompt context |
| `output_contract` | Permitted: return exactly the matching `Fast Brief`, `Fast Triage`, `Query Drafts`, or `Compact Context` format Prohibited: add recommendations, follow-up questions, or another format Stop/escalate: return one compact result and stop |

| `representative_tasks` | `Compress a long executor summary`; `Triage an unresolved item`; `Draft follow-up search queries`; `Normalize prompt context` |

| `role_boundary` | Permitted: implement exactly one assigned task through strict RED to GREEN to REFACTOR Prohibited: read `tasks.md` for more work, execute other tasks, or sequence follow-up work Stop/escalate: fail the task and return ambiguity to the parent orchestrator |
| `authorization_boundaries` | Permitted: create or modify implementation and tests needed for the assigned task Prohibited: broaden scope, approve sequencing, or decide a gate Stop/escalate: return scope or sequence questions to the parent Approval conditions and authorized approver: the parent orchestrator owns sequencing and clarification; no platform approver is named |
| `safety_requirements` | Permitted: write tests first, require a real assertion failure for RED, and keep verification green after refactor Prohibited: bypass TDD, invoke `$grill-me`, or research mechanical tasks Stop/escalate: return unresolved ambiguity through clarification or gate failure |
| `grounding_requirements` | Permitted: use the spec, plan, assigned task, completed-task context, and project patterns first, researching only concrete external unknowns with citations Prohibited: perform unnecessary research or assert unsupported external claims Stop/escalate: report an unresolved concrete unknown when evidence is insufficient |
| `mutation_policy` | Permitted: mutate only files required by the single assigned task Prohibited: execute other tasks or perform unrelated cleanup Stop/escalate: fail and report when ambiguity prevents task-scoped implementation |
| `tool_requirements` | Permitted: use supplied project commands and single-file tests, discovering commands only when missing, and use research capabilities only for genuine unknowns Prohibited: omit RED or GREEN verification Stop/escalate: return command or evidence gaps to the parent |
| `skill_requirements` | Permitted: follow the prompt-provided TDD protocol Prohibited: invoke `$grill-me` or an unrelated workflow Stop/escalate: return interactive or out-of-scope needs to the parent |
| `mcp_requirements` | Permitted: select an MCP only for a genuine task-fit unknown and retain explicit fallbacks Prohibited: infer a required MCP for mechanical implementation Stop/escalate: report missing evidence or use the documented fallback |
| `sandbox_expectations` | Permitted: write within the assigned task under `workspace-write` Prohibited: mutate outside the single-task scope or infer external-write authority Stop/escalate: return any broader access requirement to the parent |
| `output_contract` | Permitted: return `## Task Result: <TASK_ID>` with test counts, real RED/GREEN/REFACTOR evidence, commands, changed files, and errors Prohibited: recommend follow-up work or omit reproducible evidence Stop/escalate: return ambiguity and errors explicitly |
| `supported_client_assumptions` | Permitted: assume only the installed Codex TOML custom-agent runtime referenced by the source Prohibited: claim independent CLI, desktop-app, App Server, or non-interactive support Stop/escalate: require surface-specific evidence before making any broader client claim |
| `representative_tasks` | `Implement one contract, unit, or integration task under strict TDD`; `Use prior completed-task outputs`; `Report reproducible RED and GREEN evidence` |

## Claude-derived parity-role semantic contracts

The two parity roles have semantic contracts but no Codex production route. Claude frontmatter, model/tool-list syntax, colors, turn limits, and transport mechanics are excluded. Every mapping names the exact semantic source at the pinned revision and records whether the meaning is preserved, adapted to Codex-neutral language, or not applicable.

## Evidence-supported candidate catalog

The catalog includes each immutable production baseline plus the roadmap's one named role-level starting model. `gpt-5.6-sol` / `medium` is the project hypothesis for the three demanding executors; `gpt-5.6-terra` / `medium` is the hypothesis for structured, research, analyst, and parity work. `autopilot-fast-helper` already has the one project-evidenced fast tuple, so its unchanged production baseline is its only route candidate. Additional catalog models are platform facts but do not yet have a project-level role-and-effort tuple; the roadmap makes their challenger status conditional on G56R-002 discovery. This is an evidence boundary, not an exclusion.

`PROJ-ROADMAP-START` is the tracked project-fact record for the
**Candidate-route starting hypotheses** table in
`docs/ai/specs/codex-gpt-5-6-agent-routing-technical-roadmap.md` at pinned
revision `7f8c17369e15a359c6e24eea844c79a2d47d3bab`. It establishes only the
role-level starting hypothesis, not executability, qualification, preference,
or fallback order.

Every candidate is project-eligible, installation availability is `unresolved_g56r_002`, treatment is `unchanged`, and qualification is `unqualified`. No hard-contract incompatibility or dominance exclusion is evidenced. Lexical presentation has no preference or fallback meaning.

| Agent | Candidate route ID | Model / effort / treatment | Basis and evidence | State |

| `analyze-executor` | `candidate-route/analyze-executor/gpt-5-5/xhigh/unchanged/v1` | `gpt-5.5` / `xhigh` / `unchanged` | Immutable source comparator; `PROJ-ROUTE-ANALYZE-EXECUTOR` | eligible; availability unresolved; unqualified |
| `analyze-executor` | `candidate-route/analyze-executor/gpt-5-6-sol/medium/unchanged/v1` | `gpt-5.6-sol` / `medium` / `unchanged` | Unqualified roadmap hypothesis; `PROJ-ROADMAP-START`, `OAI-20260714-MODELS`, `OAI-20260714-REASONING` | eligible; availability unresolved; unqualified |

No prompt or context variant is cataloged: the reviewed project evidence proposes no bounded-overhead variant at this stage. Therefore each tuple's unchanged treatment is both the candidate and attribution control, and there is no variant without a control.

## Fixture contracts and backlog

Exactly three fixture directories exist. They remain historical prompt emulation and therefore `non_release_evidence`; `current` means only that the repository fixture contract exists. The other nine contracts make the G56R-003 backlog executable without fabricating files in this spike.

| Agent | Status / path | Representative input and task | Expected behavior and output |
|-------|---------------|-------------------------------|------------------------------|
| `analyze-executor` | `missing`; null; `non_release_evidence` | one analysis-remediation task with feature artifacts and command; Run analyze and remediate all severities | Exercise analyze-executor while preserving all twelve bound semantic fields, including prohibited behavior and stop or escalation conditions. Output: `Analyze Result` with finding counts, cited remediations, verification, unresolved items, and errors. |
| `autopilot-fast-helper` | `missing`; null; `non_release_evidence` | one bounded prompt-only compression or triage packet; Compress a long executor summary | Exercise autopilot-fast-helper while preserving all twelve bound semantic fields, including prohibited behavior and stop or escalation conditions. Output: exactly one `Fast Brief`, `Fast Triage`, `Query Drafts`, or `Compact Context`. |
| `checklist-executor` | `missing`; null; `non_release_evidence` | one named checklist domain with feature artifacts and command; Generate one domain checklist | Exercise checklist-executor while preserving all twelve bound semantic fields, including prohibited behavior and stop or escalation conditions. Output: `Checklist Domain Result` with counts, cited gap fixes, verification, unresolved items, and errors. |
| `clarify-executor` | `missing`; null; `non_release_evidence` | workflow and specification context for ambiguity analysis; Find material specification ambiguities | Exercise clarify-executor while preserving all twelve bound semantic fields, including prohibited behavior and stop or escalation conditions. Output: `Clarify Question Set` with at most five evidence-backed questions and suggested sections. |
| `codebase-analyst` | `current`; `tests/speckit-pro/layer6-efficiency/fixtures-codex/codebase-analyst`; `non_release_evidence` | one consensus question requiring repository-pattern evidence; Find existing batch-error patterns | Exercise codebase-analyst while preserving all twelve bound semantic fields, including prohibited behavior and stop or escalation conditions. Output: `Answer`, one to five repository evidence records, and confidence. |
| `consensus-synthesizer` | `missing`; null; `non_release_evidence` | one to three normalized analyst responses, category, and round; Synthesize a single category-routed answer | Exercise consensus-synthesizer while preserving all twelve bound semantic fields, including prohibited behavior and stop or escalation conditions. Every fixture contract asserts all twelve FR-006 fields: `role_boundary`, `authorization_boundaries`, `safety_requirements`, `grounding_requirements`, `mutation_policy`, `tool_requirements`, `skill_requirements`, `mcp_requirements`, `sandbox_expectations`, `output_contract`, `supported_client_assumptions`, and `representative_tasks`.
